      import { db } from '@/lib/db';
      import { resolveRetentionDays, retentionCutoff } from './settings';
      import { sweepOrphanScreenshotFiles } from '@/lib/screenshots/sweep';
      import { removeArtifactByPath } from '@/lib/storage';
      import { BREAK_TITLES } from '@/lib/breaks/service';
      
      export interface RetentionResult {
        screenshots: number;
        activities: number;
        reports: number;
        aiInsights: number;
        sentimentRecords: number;
        auditLogsAnonymized: number;
        consentLogsAnonymized: number;
        /** USB device events purged past the org's usb_event_retention_days. */
        usbEvents: number;
        /** Policy violations purged past the org's policy_violation_retention_days. */
        policyViolations: number;
        /** Read/archived notifications purged past notification_retention_days. */
        notifications: number;
        /** Resolved/archived alerts purged past alert_retention_days. */
        alerts: number;
        /** Ended BreakSession rows purged past break_session_retention_days (active breaks are never purged). */
        breakSessions: number;
        /** Legacy "Break Mode …" Activity mirror rows purged with their sessions. */
        breakActivityRows: number;
        /** Physical screenshot files with no matching DB row, removed this run. */
        orphanScreenshotsRemoved: number;
        /** Paths whose physical artifact could NOT be deleted this run — the DB row
         * is deliberately retained so a later run can retry the unlink. */
        fileErrors: string[];
        /** Audio recordings and transcriptions purged past retention. */
        audioRecordings: number;
        audioTranscriptions: number;
        audioFileErrors: string[];
        /** Organization-level failures (continue-on-error isolation in runRetention). */
        errors: string[];
      }
      
      const EMPTY: RetentionResult = {
        screenshots: 0,
        activities: 0,
        reports: 0,
        aiInsights: 0,
        sentimentRecords: 0,
        auditLogsAnonymized: 0,
        consentLogsAnonymized: 0,
        usbEvents: 0,
        policyViolations: 0,
        notifications: 0,
        alerts: 0,
        breakSessions: 0,
        breakActivityRows: 0,
        audioRecordings: 0,
        audioTranscriptions: 0,
        audioFileErrors: [],
        orphanScreenshotsRemoved: 0,
        fileErrors: [],
        errors: [],
      };
      
      function mergeInto(target: RetentionResult, other: RetentionResult): RetentionResult {
        for (const key of Object.keys(EMPTY) as (keyof RetentionResult)[]) {
          if (key === 'fileErrors' || key === 'errors' || key === 'audioFileErrors') {
            target[key] = [...target[key], ...other[key]];
          } else {
            target[key] += other[key];
          }
        }
        return target;
      }
      
      /**
       * Attempt to remove a stored artifact through the active storage driver
       * (local filesystem, or Supabase Storage on Vercel). Screenshot paths map to
       * driver keys; any other artifact type is only meaningful on the local
       * filesystem and is unlinked directly (legacy report files). Returns true
       * when the artifact is confirmed gone (deleted, or already absent); false on
       * any real failure — the caller must then KEEP the DB row so cleanup retries.
       */
      async function removeFile(orgId: string, filePath: string, kind: 'screenshot' | 'legacy'): Promise<boolean> {
        return removeArtifactByPath(orgId, filePath, kind);
      }
      
      /**
       * Two-phase purge of a file-backed table: unlink the physical artifact FIRST
       * and only delete the DB row for artifacts that are confirmed gone. A file
       * that cannot be deleted keeps its row (retryable next run) and is reported
       * in result.fileErrors — we never report a purge while the artifact remains.
       */

export async function finishJob(job: string, error?: string, lastResult?: Record<string, unknown> | null): Promise<void> {
  await db.jobRun.update({
    where: { job },
    data: {
      status: error ? 'failed' : 'completed',
      finishedAt: new Date(),
      lastRunAt: new Date(),
      lastError: error ?? null,
      lastResult: lastResult ? JSON.stringify(lastResult) : null,
    },
  });
}

      "oldString": "import type { ApiClient } from './client';\nimport type { ActivityRecord, ActivityUploadResponse } from '../types/api';\n\nexport class ActivityApi {\n  constructor(private readonly client: ApiClient) {}\n\n  /** Upload a batch of activities (backend caps at 100 per request). */\n  upload(activities: ActivityRecord[]): Promise<ActivityUploadResponse> {\n    if (activities.length === 0) {\n      return Promise.resolve({ success: true, count: 0, message: 'No activities' });\n    }\n    return this.client.post<ActivityUploadResponse>('/api/agent/activity', { activities });\n  }\n}",

Both fields\n * are additive: older servers ignore them, and omitting them keeps the exact\n * legacy payload shape.\n */\nexport interface ActivityUploadMeta {\n  batchId: string;\n  batchSeq: number;\n}\n\nexport class ActivityApi {\n  constructor(private readonly client: ApiClient) {}\n\n  /** Upload a batch of activities (backend caps at 100 per request). */\n  upload(activities: ActivityRecord[], meta?: ActivityUploadMeta): Promise<ActivityUploadResponse> {\n    if (activities.length === 0) {\n      return Promise.resolve({ success: true, count: 0, message: 'No activities' });\n    }\n    const payload: Record<string, unknown> = { activities };\n    if (meta) {\n      payload.batchId = meta.batchId;\n      payload.batchSeq = meta.batchSeq;\n    }\n    return this.client.post<ActivityUploadResponse>('/api/agent/activity', payload);\n  }\n}",
      "allowMultiple": false
    }
  ]
}

      "oldString": "import type { ActivityApi } from '../api/activity';\nimport type { ApiClientError } from '../api/client';\nimport { logger } from '../lib/logger';\nimport type { ActivityQueue, QueuedActivity } from '../storage/activity-queue';",
      "newString": "import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';\nimport type { ActivityApi, ActivityUploadMeta } from '../api/activity';\nimport type { ApiClientError } from '../api/client';\nimport { logger } from '../lib/logger';\nimport type { ActivityQueue, QueuedActivity } from '../storage/activity-queue';",
      "allowMultiple": false
    },
    {
      "oldString": "export interface QueueUploadStats {\n  uploaded: number;\n  failed: number;\n  skipped: number;\n}",
      "newString": "export interface QueueUploadStats {\n  uploaded: number;\n  failed: number;\n  skipped: number;\n}\n\n// Fixed 16-byte namespace for deterministic UUID v5 batch ids (arbitrary but\n// constant — every agent/process derives the SAME id for the same item set).\nconst BATCH_ID_NAMESPACE = Buffer.from('a5d0b3a27f5c4c9a9a318f0e0c1a2b3c', 'hex');\n\n/**\n * Deterministic UUID v5 over the batch's queued item ids.\n *\n * One batchId per logical upload batch (never per row). Because it is derived\n * from the item ids themselves, the SAME queued set always yields the SAME\n * batchId — a retry after a crash or a lost response (the same head-of-queue\n * items are re-drained) is idempotent server-side instead of duplicating\n * rows. A different item set (e.g. after a partial ack) yields a different\n * id, so receipts can never swallow unrelated uploads.\n */\nexport function deriveBatchId(itemIds: string[]): string {\n  const digest = createHash('sha1')\n    .update(BATCH_ID_NAMESPACE)\n    .update([...itemIds].sort().join('|'), 'utf8')\n    .digest();\n  digest[6] = (digest[6] & 0x0f) | 0x50; // version 5\n  digest[8] = (digest[8] & 0x3f) | 0x80; // RFC 4122 variant (10xx)\n  const hex = digest.subarray(0, 16).toString('hex');\n  return `${hex.slice(0, 8)}-${hex.slice(8, 12)}-${hex.slice(12, 16)}-${hex.slice(16, 20)}-${hex.slice(20)}`;\n}",
      "allowMultiple": false
    },
    {
      "oldString": "export class QueueUploader {\n  private lastRunAt: number | null = null;\n  private lastError: string | null = null;",
      "newString": "export class QueueUploader {\n  private lastRunAt: number | null = null;\n  private lastError: string | null = null;\n  /** Monotonic per-process upload counter (batchSeq payload field). */\n  private batchSeq = 0;",
      "allowMultiple": false
    },
    {
      "oldString": "  /** One upload attempt: send the batch and ack only after server confirm. */\n  private async attemptUpload(batch: QueuedActivity[]): Promise<void> {\n    await this.api.upload(batch.map((i) => i.record));\n    await this.queue.ack(batch);\n    this.lastError = null;\n  }",
      "newString": "  /** One upload attempt: send the batch and ack only after server confirm. */\n  private async attemptUpload(batch: QueuedActivity[]): Promise<void> {\n    const meta: ActivityUploadMeta = {\n      // Stable across retries of the same queued items (see deriveBatchId) so\n      // at-least-once redelivery dedupes server-side. batchSeq increments per\n      // upload attempt — informational only.\n      batchId: deriveBatchId(batch.map((i) => i.id)),\n      batchSeq: ++this.batchSeq,\n    };\n    await this.api.upload(batch.map((i) => i.record), meta);\n    await this.queue.ack(batch);\n    this.lastError = null;\n  }",
      "allowMultiple": false
    }
  ]
}

test('F-01: consecutive drain ticks both 401 — the batch survives BOTH (no cross-tick loss)', async () => {
  const dir = await freshDir();
  try {
    const queue = new ActivityQueue(dir);
    await queue.enqueue(record);
    await queue.enqueue({ ...record, title: 'b.ts' });
    const api = new FakeActivityApi();
    api.script = [401, 401]; // both ticks return 401
    const up = new QueueUploader(queue, api, async () => false); // recovery never succeeds

    const first = await up.drain(); // 401 → recovery fails → retain, stop
    assert.equal(first.uploaded, 0);
    assert.equal(queue.length, 2, 'tick 1 retains the batch');

    const second = await up.drain(); // 401 again → retained again
    assert.equal(second.uploaded, 0);
    assert.equal(queue.length, 2, 'tick 2 retains the SAME batch — nothing lost across drain ticks');
    assert.equal(api.uploadCalls, 2, 'one attempt per tick, no intra-tick looping');
  } finally {
    await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
  }
});

> 11. Cross-repo contract: static checks — reads sibling agent repo files (AGENT_ROOT) to verify ActivityApi.upload sends batchId/batchSeq + deriveBatchId deterministic; and web route accepts absence. If sibling absent → skip (like branding-regression AGENT_PRESENT pattern).

agent: no batchId → accepted, legacy response shape (no\n *     deduplicated key), rows inserted\n *   - tenant isolation: same batchId in different orgs does NOT dedupe\n *   - employee isolation: the agent tree (when present) sends batchId +\n *     batchSeq and derives a stable batch id (static contract check)\n *\n * Run: npx tsx --test tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts\n */\nimport { test, agentTokenFor(employeeId: string): Promise<string> {\n  const token = randomBytes(48).toString('hex');\n  const employee = await db.employee.findUnique({ where: { id: employeeId } });\n  if (!employee) throw new Error(`Employee not found: ${employeeId}`);\n  await db.agentToken.create({\n    data: {\n      token,\n      employee: { connect: { id: employeeId } },\n      organization: { connect: { id: employee.organizationId } },\n      expiresAt: new Date(Date.now() + 3600_000),\n    },\n  });\n  return token;\n}\n\n/** Enable/disable the org-scoped activity_dedupe flag. */\nasync 'activity_dedupe' } },\n  });\n  if (existing) {\n    await db.organizationSetting.update({ where: { id: existing.id }, data: { value: String(enabled) } });\n  } else {\n    await Deterministic RFC-4122-shaped id for tests (validated by the route regex).\n  return seed.padEnd(36, '0').slice(0, 36).replace(/^(.{8})(.{4})(.{4})(.{4})(.{12})$/, '$1-$2-4$3-$4-$5');\n}\n\nfunction 'AD-EMP-B1');\n  await Promise.all([\n    grantConsent(empA1.id, orgA.id, 'activity_tracking'),\n    grantConsent(empA2.id, orgA.id, 'activity_tracking'),\n    grantConsent(empB1.id, orgB.id, 'activity_tracking'),\n  ]);\n  tokenA1 = await agentTokenFor(empA1.id);\n  tokenA2 = await agentTokenFor(empA2.id);\n  tokenB1 = await agentTokenFor(empB1.id);\n});\n\nafter(async () => {\n  await db.$disconnect();\n  if (process.env.ACTIVITY_DEDUPE_TEST_MIGRATED_DB !== '1') {\n    try {\n      execSync(`node scripts/pg-test-db.mjs drop ${TEST_DB_NAME}`, {\n        env: { ...process.env, db.activityBatchReceipt.count({ where: { organizationId: orgId, employeeId, batchId } });\n}\n\n// ─── P1-1 Basic ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ntest('P1-1: batch with assert.equal(body.deduplicated, 0, 'fresh batch — nothing deduplicated');\n  assert.equal(await countActivities(empA1.id), 3);\n  const receipt = await db.activityBatchReceipt.findUnique({\n    where: {\n      organizationId_employeeId_batchId: { organizationId: orgA.id, employeeId: empA1.id, batchId: BATCH_1 },\n    },\n  });\n  assert.ok(receipt, 'receipt row created');\n  assert.equal(receipt!.rowCount, 3, 'receipt records the accepted row count');\n});\n\n// ─── P1-2 Duplicate ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ntest('P1-2: same org+employee+batchId twice → zero duplicates, success + deduplicated count', async () => {\n  const res = await route.POST(\n    req(tokenA1, { method: 'POST', url: activityUrl(), body: { activities: number; deduplicated: number };\n  assert.equal(body.success, true);\n  assert.equal(body.count, 0, 'no new rows from the replay');\n  assert.equal(body.deduplicated, 3, 'reports the number of records deduplicated');\n  assert.equal(await countActivities(empA1.id), 3, 'still exactly the original three rows');\n  assert.equal(await countReceipts(orgA.id, empA1.id, BATCH_1), 1, 'exactly one at-least-once)');\n  assert.equal('deduplicated' in body, false, 'no dedupe reporting when the flag is off');\n  assert.equal(await countReceipts(orgA.id, empA1.id, batchId), 0, 'no receipts written with 422', async () => {\n  const bad = [\n    { activities: activityBatch(1), batchId: 'not-a-uuid' },\n    { activities: activityBatch(1), batchId: '12345678-1234-1234-1234-1234567890ab' }, // wrong length\n    { activities: activityBatch(1), batchId: BATCH_X, batchSeq: -1 },\n    { activities: activityBatch(1), batchId: BATCH_X, batchSeq: 1.5 },\n  ];\n  for (const body of bad) {\n    const res assert.equal(await countReceipts(orgA.id, empA1.id, BATCH_X), 0);\n});\n\n// ─── P1-10 Retention ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\ntest('P1-10: stale receipts are purged by the activity retention sweep; fresh kept', async () => {\n  // Fresh receipts exist under org A (BATCH_1 for empA1). > _Reasoning_
> Build passes. Now performance review (§19) — reasoned analysis:
> - Receipt lookup on duplicate: findUnique on (organizationId, employeeId, batchId) → unique index → O(1) b-tree probe, no scan.
> - Unique conflict handling: unique constraint is the arbiter; loser transaction aborts and re-reads one row by unique key — bounded.
> - Activity insertion unchanged (createMany with existing indexes).
> - Retention deletion: indexed on (organizationId, receivedAt) — deleteMany org-scoped with receivedAt range uses index; bounded per org; runs per-org in the scheduled sweep with limit pattern.
> - Receipt growth: one row per activity upload batch (max 100 rows/batch). Compare scale: 1 batch per drain ~= activity volume / avg batch size. At §L scale (100 employees, ~1 activity/min during 8h): 100×480 = 48k activities/day; batch ≤100 rows → ≥480 receipts/day per org... upper bound roughly activity rows/2 if avg batch=2? Realistic drain every minute with ≤100 items → few hundred receipts/day/org; 90-day retention → ~43k rows max per org (if 480/day). Negligible vs Activity rows (~4.3M/90d). Documented.
> 
> Let me quantify in report text: growth ≈ uploads/day. Fine.
> 
> Also update the agent's F-13 comment in queue-uploader.ts? The F-13 doc says "a client-only key the server ignores would be cosmetic" — now outdated: the server DOES implement idempotency (when the org enables activity_dedupe). Update comment to reflect Phase-1 reality to keep docs truthful. Also the class doc F-01 unchanged.
> 
> Update F-13 comment block.

{
  "path": "docs/PHASE-1-REPORT.md",
  "instructions": "Write the Phase 1 reliable-activity-telemetry report with evidence.",
  "content": "# PHASE 1 REPORT — RELIABLE ACTIVITY TELEMETRY (batch idempotency)\n\n**Status: GREEN** — verified 2026-09-03 on `omnisight-web` and `omnisight-agent`.\n**Phase 0 gate:** GREEN (see `docs/PHASE-0-REPORT.md`) before any Phase 1 code was written.\n**Evidence logs:** `p1-suite-{1..5}.log`, `/tmp/dedupe2.log`, `/tmp/build-p1.log`, `/tmp/tc2.log`, `/tmp/lint3.log`, `/tmp/agent-*.log`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Summary\n\nActivity uploads can no longer duplicate rows when an agent retries or replays a batch — **but only when the organization opts in**, preserving today's behavior everywhere else:\n\n- **Agent** (`omnisight-agent`) now attaches `batchId` + `batchSeq` to every activity upload. The `batchId` is a deterministic UUID v5 over the batch's queued-item ids: ONE id per logical upload batch (never per row), and — critically — the SAME queued items always derive the SAME id, so a crash between a successful server write and the local ack replays with the identical id.\n- **Web** (`omnisight-web`) accepts the optional `batchId`. When the org-scoped `activity_dedupe` flag is enabled, a receipt (`ActivityBatchReceipt`) is written in the SAME transaction as the activity rows; a replay hits the unique `(organizationId, employeeId, batchId)` key and returns a successful response with an accurate `deduplicated` count instead of inserting again.\n- **Backward compatibility:** uploads without `batchId` (old agents) and orgs with the flag off keep byte-for-byte legacy behavior. Phase 1 does not require any agent upgrade.\n- Deliberate scope decision: **no `agent_min_version` gate was added** (see §9) — nothing in Phase 1 needs a newer agent, and enforcement would risk breaking older agents for zero benefit.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Architecture\n\n```\nAgent drain (QueueUploader)\n  queue.peekBatch(≤100) ──► deriveBatchId(item ids)  (stable UUID v5 per batch)\n                            + batchSeq (monotonic per process)\n  POST /api/agent/activity { activities, batchId, batchSeq }\n\nWeb route\n  validate token + consent + items (unchanged, all legacy rules)\n  if batchId absent OR org activity_dedupe off  ──► legacy createMany (unchanged)\n  else\n    db.$transaction(\n      tx.activityBatchReceipt.create({ org, employee, batchId, rowCount })\n      tx.activity.createMany(rows)\n    )\n    on P2002 (unique org+employee+batchId) ──► read winner's receipt → 200 { count:0, deduplicated:N }\n```\n\nThe receipt is written with the rows (atomic) so neither \"receipt without rows\" nor \"rows without receipt\" can exist. The unique constraint, not application locking, arbitrates races.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Schema (additive)\n\nNew model in `prisma/schema.prisma`:\n\n```prisma\nmodel ActivityBatchReceipt {\n  id             String   @id @default(cuid())\n  organizationId String\n  employeeId     String\n  batchId        String\n  receivedAt     DateTime @default(now())\n  rowCount       Int\n  organization   Organization @relation(...)  // onDelete: Cascade\n  employee       Employee     @relation(...)  // onDelete: Cascade\n  @@unique([organizationId, employeeId, batchId])\n  @@index([organizationId, receivedAt])\n  @@index([employeeId])\n  @@index([receivedAt])\n}\n```\n\n- Migration: `prisma/migrations/20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts/migration.sql` (new table + unique + 3 indexes + 2 FKs; no column/table altered, no backfill).\n- Verified: `prisma validate` PASS; `prisma migrate deploy` on a scratch DB PASS; `prisma migrate diff --from-migrations → schema` reports **No difference detected** (hand-written SQL matches Prisma's expectation exactly).\n- Receipt rows have their own `organizationId` FK (unlike `Activity`, which scopes through the employee) — the table is a first-class tenant-scoped record.\n\n---\n\n## 4. API contract\n\n`POST /api/agent/activity` — request (both fields OPTIONAL):\n\n| Field | Type | Rule |\n|---|---|---|\n| `activities` | array | unchanged (≤100, validated as before) |\n| `batchId` | string | RFC-4122 UUID v1–v5; malformed → `422 { error }` |\n| `batchSeq` | number | non-negative safe integer; malformed → `422`; informational, not persisted |\n\nResponses:\n\n| Scenario | Status | Body |\n|---|---|---|\n| No `batchId` (legacy agent) | 200 | `{ success, count, message }` — unchanged, no `deduplicated` key |\n| `batchId` + flag off | 200 | same legacy shape (key ignored) |\n| `batchId` + flag on, fresh | 200 | `{ success, count: N, deduplicated: 0, message }` |\n| `batchId` + flag on, replay | 200 | `{ success, count: 0, deduplicated: <rowCount>, message }` |\n| Consent missing | 403 | unchanged |\n| Website rows while tracking off | 403 | unchanged (whole batch atomic) |\n| Invalid batch metadata | 422 | new |\n\nServer-side note: `batchSeq` is validated but intentionally not stored (the schema does not carry it; it exists for ordering/debug observability).\n\n---\n\n## 5. Compatibility behavior\n\n- **Old agents** (no `batchId`): accepted; exact legacy ingestion path; duplicates under at-least-once remain possible exactly as before (documented F-13). No agent upgrade is required by Phase 1 — proven by test P1-5 and the runtime contract tests.\n- **Old servers** (no dedupe support): ignore `batchId`/`batchSeq` (unknown JSON fields); the agent keeps working against them.\n- **Orgs that never enable `activity_dedupe`**: receipt table stays empty; ingestion is untouched.\n- Feature flag default **OFF** — no behavior change until an org opts in (OrganizationSetting key `activity_dedupe` = `'true'`; server-side only, never sent to agents or shown in the agent-monitoring toggles).\n\n---\n\n## 6. Transaction strategy & race-condition analysis\n\nReceipt insert + activity `createMany` run inside one interactive `db.$transaction`. The receipt's compound unique constraint is the arbiter.\n\n| Scenario | Behavior |\n|---|---|\n| Two identical uploads simultaneously | First commits receipt+rows. Second's `create` raises P2002 inside its tx → entire tx aborts (no partial rows, Postgres semantics) → handler re-reads the committed receipt → `200 deduplicated: N`. No duplicate rows, no error to either client. |\n| Retry after server response lost | First tx committed; the retry follows the duplicate path → `200`, zero new rows. |\n| Agent process crash before local ack | Items remain at the queue head with their original ids → the next drain derives the SAME `batchId` → server dedupes. |\n| Partial batch validation failure | Validation happens before any write → whole batch `422`; no receipt, no rows. |\n| Same `batchId`, different tenant/employee | Scoped by the unique key + server-derived ids → independent receipts, no cross-tenant/employee dedupe (proven by P1-6/P1-7). |\n\nThe duplicate path is idempotent and read-only after the first commit; the only extra work on replay is one unique-index probe + one `findUnique` read.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Agent changes\n\n- `src/api/activity.ts` — `upload(activities, meta?: ActivityUploadMeta)`; attaches `batchId`/`batchSeq` when meta is supplied (payload shape otherwise identical). New exported `ActivityUploadMeta`.\n- `src/services/queue-uploader.ts` — new exported `deriveBatchId(itemIds)` (deterministic UUID v5 over sorted queued-item ids; one per batch, stable across retries/crash-replay, different for different item sets) and a monotonic per-process `batchSeq`. `attemptUpload` now sends the meta on every attempt, including 401-recovery retries of the same batch.\n- `src/types/api.ts` — `ActivityUploadResponse.deduplicated?: number` (additive; old servers omit it).\n- `tests/queue-uploader.test.ts` — +2 tests (P1-1 deriveBatchId determinism/shape; P1-2 upload carries one stable batchId + monotonic batchSeq across a failed-then-successful drain); fake API extended to capture meta. F-13 doc comment updated to describe the Phase-1 dedupe contract.\n\nResult: agent typecheck PASS, **627/627 tests PASS** (baseline was 625; +2 new), build PASS.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Tests executed (exact commands & results)\n\n### Web (`omnisight-web`)\n- New suite `tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts` — 11 tests, all PASS (`node --import tsx --test tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts`):\n  - P1-1 basic accept + receipt + exact row count\n  - P1-2 duplicate → 0 rows, 200, `deduplicated: 3`\n  - P1-3 concurrent identical uploads (Promise.all) → one row set, one receipt, both clients 200\n  - P1-4 committed-then-replayed batch → no growth\n  - P1-5 old agent (no batchId) → legacy behavior + legacy response shape, replayed duplicate inserts as before\n  - P1-6 tenant isolation (same batchId, org B) → independent\n  - P1-7 employee isolation (same batchId, same org, employee 2) → independent\n  - P1-8 flag off → batchId ignored, legacy duplicate allowed, no receipts\n  - P1-9 malformed batchId/batchSeq → 422, nothing written\n  - P1-10 retention sweep purges stale receipts (window) and keeps fresh ones\n  - P1-11 cross-repo static contract (agent tree present): upload attaches batchId+batchSeq; deriveBatchId over item ids; retry stability documented\n- Targeted regression re-runs (activity route + retention consumers): `website-tracking`, `website-100`, `agent-hardening`, `agent-cross-org-attack`, `consent-summary`, `super-admin`, `audio`, `anomaly-hardening`, `break-hardening`, `hardening` — all exit 0.\n- **Full web suite: 97/97 suites pass, 1573 subtests pass, 0 fail** (5 sequential chunks; same per-file command `scripts/run-tests.mjs` spawns).\n- `npm run typecheck` PASS · `npm run lint` 0 errors (439 warnings, unchanged class) · `npm run build` PASS.\n\n### Agent (`omnisight-agent`)\n- `npm run typecheck` PASS · `npm test` 627/627 PASS · `npm run build` PASS.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Performance review (Phase 1 §19)\n\n- **Receipt lookup / duplicate path:** unique index on `(organizationId, employeeId, batchId)` → the race-loser does one b-tree probe + one `findUnique` (primary key of the unique index). No table scan; cost is O(1)-ish per replay and zero in steady state.\n- **Unique-conflict handling:** Postgres enforces the constraint; the aborted transaction is not reused (Prisma re-read happens on a fresh statement after the tx aborts), so no \"current transaction is aborted\" pitfalls.\n- **Activity insertion:** unchanged `createMany`; existing indexes (`employeeId, timestamp, category`, `createdAt`, …) unaffected.\n- **Retention deletion:** `deleteMany` scoped `organizationId` + `receivedAt < cutoff` uses the `(organizationId, receivedAt)` index; the scheduled org sweep already batches per-org. No new unbounded scans.\n- **Receipt growth:** one receipt per accepted upload batch (≤100 rows each), purged on the activity window (default 90 days; 0 = never). Worst-case bound ≈ upload-batch volume — e.g. at the audit's scale model (100 employees × ~1 activity/min × 8 h), activity ≈ 48k rows/day and receipts ≈ hundreds/day/org → ~45k receipts per org at 90 days vs ~4.3M activity rows: negligible (~1%). Retention prevents unbounded growth.\n- No new external services (no Redis/Kafka/etc.); PostgreSQL remains the system of record.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Rollback strategy\n\n1. **Disable the flag:** delete/set the `OrganizationSetting` row `activity_dedupe` → ingestion returns to legacy behavior immediately; receipts stop being written and are no longer consulted (existing receipt rows are inert).\n2. **Revert code:** revert the route, retention, settings and agent changes (working-tree diff; nothing committed in this phase).\n3. **Revert the migration:** `20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts` is a plain additive `CREATE TABLE` — a rollback is a single `DROP TABLE \"ActivityBatchReceipt\"` (no data transformation, no existing rows touched, Activity data untouched).\n4. Old agents/old servers are unaffected at every step (optional fields).\n\n---\n\n## 11. Files changed (this phase)\n\n**omnisight-web**\n- `prisma/schema.prisma` — `ActivityBatchReceipt` model + back-relations on `Organization`/`Employee`\n- `prisma/migrations/20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts/migration.sql` — additive table/indexes/FKs\n- `src/app/api/agent/activity/route.ts` — optional `batchId`/`batchSeq` validation + transactional receipt dedupe + `deduplicated` reporting\n- `src/lib/jobs/settings.ts` — `ACTIVITY_DEDUPE_SETTING_KEY` + `resolveActivityDedupeEnabled` (org-scoped, default off, server-only)\n- `src/lib/jobs/retention.ts` — receipt purge on the activity window + `RetentionResult.activityBatchReceipts`\n- `src/lib/jobs/run.ts` — empty retention result now includes `activityBatchReceipts`\n- `tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts` — new integration suite (11 tests incl. cross-repo contract)\n\n**omnisight-agent**\n- `src/api/activity.ts` — optional `ActivityUploadMeta`, payload attachment\n- `src/services/queue-uploader.ts` — `deriveBatchId` + `batchSeq`, meta on every attempt, updated F-13 contract note\n- `src/types/api.ts` — `deduplicated?: number` on the upload response\n- `tests/queue-uploader.test.ts` — 2 new tests + fake capture\n\n---\n\n## 12. Security & privacy impact\n\n- **Security:** no weakening. batchId is scoped server-side to the authenticated token's (organization, employee) — never taken from client claims; cross-tenant/employee reuse is impossible by construction (unique key) and proven by tests. Batch metadata is validated (UUID shape, integer bounds). No new secrets; receipts contain no activity content (id, tenant, batch key, count, time).\n- **Privacy:** no new collection — batch metadata is not activity content and stores no titles/URLs/domains. Retention applies (activity window). Consent/break/working-hours gates are untouched (route changes happen strictly after the existing gates).\n\n---\n\n## 13. Remaining risks / notes\n\n- Duplicate suppression is **opt-in per org** (`activity_dedupe`). Orgs that leave the flag off retain the documented at-least-once semantics; a future phase (or ops) can enable it org-wide. An admin-facing toggle is intentionally out of Phase 1 scope (server-side flag today).\n- `batchSeq` is per-process monotonic (resets on agent restart) — sufficient for its informational purpose; if ordering across restarts is ever needed it must be persisted.\n- `agent_min_version` was deliberately **not** added (§1, §16 condition): the existing settings registry is agent-facing config, no Phase 1 consumer needs a version gate, and enforcing a minimum would break the \"no agent upgrade required\" guarantee. It can be added additively later if a phase needs a newer collector.\n- Realtime/workday/productivity math that consumes `Activity` rows now benefits from dedupe once flags are on; Phases 3/4 will rely on this.\n"
}

Both Phase 0 and Phase 1 are complete and **GREEN** across both repositories, with executable evidence and reports.

## What was done

**Phase 0 — Stabilization** (`docs/PHASE-0-REPORT.md`)
- A prior session's fixes were audited and re-verified fresh: the shared `req()` helper no longer produces `GET`+body (Next 16), 13 stale/broken suites corrected, security adjudication documented (anonymous registration → 422 `AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`; existing-device denial → uniform 404), `.next/dev/types` clean wired into `typecheck`/`build`, bun-canonical lockfile (both stub `package-lock.json`s removed), `.claude` lint exclusion, CI rewritten to reproduce the full gate.
- Found and fixed one *new* pre-existing flake: `audio.test.ts` crashed node:test's runner ~33% of the time (`Unable to deserialize cloned data`); root cause was `console.log` noise racing the IPC channel at exit. Logs removed → 10/10 stable.
- Removed remaining hardcoded credentials (`tests/rbac-runtime-verification.mjs`, `scripts/capture-docs-screenshots.mjs` now env-driven).
- **Web: lint 0 errors · typecheck ✓ · build ✓ · 96/96 suites (1561 subtests) · Agent: 625/625 ✓, typecheck+build ✓**

**Phase 1 — Reliable activity telemetry** (`docs/PHASE-1-REPORT.md`)
- **Web:** additive `ActivityBatchReceipt` model + drift-free migration `20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts`; `/api/agent/activity` accepts optional `batchId`/`batchSeq`; when the org-scoped `activity_dedupe` flag (default **off**) is on, the receipt + rows commit in one transaction and replays return `200 { deduplicated: N }`; retention purges stale receipts on the activity window; receipts indexed for org/time/employee.
- **Agent:** every upload now carries a deterministic UUID-v5 `batchId` derived from the batch's queued item ids (one per batch, **stable across retries and crash-replay**) plus a monotonic `batchSeq`. Old agents/servers unaffected.
- New `tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts` (11 tests: basic, duplicate, concurrent race, retry, legacy agent, tenant + employee isolation, flag-off, validation, retention, cross-repo contract).
- **Web: 97/97 suites (1573 subtests), typecheck ✓, lint 0 errors, build ✓ · Agent: 627/627 ✓ (2 new tests), typecheck + build ✓**

Nothing is committed — all changes are in the working tree, so rollback remains a matter of not committing (per the reports' rollback sections). The next phases (2–8) remain, each gated on the previous phase staying green.

# 1. OBJECTIVE

Implement reliable activity telemetry ingestion with idempotent batch processing.

Primary objective:

> A logical activity upload that is retried, replayed after a crash, or submitted concurrently must never create duplicate Activity rows.

Secondary objectives:

* preserve compatibility with existing agents
* preserve existing activity collection behavior
* preserve existing API behavior
* preserve organization isolation
* preserve employee isolation
* make ingestion transactionally safe
* make receipt retention bounded
* establish an extensible agent capability/version contract.

Do NOT implement Phase 2+ features in this task.

Specifically DO NOT implement yet:

* screenshot thumbnails
* screenshot quotas
* WorkDaySummary
* CategoryRule
* alert rules
* report schedules
* automatic screenshot AI
* mobile Service Apps.

---

# 5. CRITICAL DESIGN REQUIREMENT

The idempotency key must be scoped to:

`organizationId + employeeId + batchId`

Never use only:

`batchId`

as the dedupe identity.

Reason:

A malicious or buggy agent from Organization A must never cause a legitimate batch from Organization B to be considered a duplicate.

Likewise:

Employee A and Employee B must not dedupe each other's batches.

---

# 6. AGENT IMPLEMENTATION

Repository:

`omnisight-agent`

Inspect the real queue uploader.

For each logical upload/drain batch, attach:

* `batchId`
* `batchSeq`

### batchId

Must be a UUID.

One logical batch gets exactly one `batchId`.

Do NOT generate a new UUID for each Activity row.

Do NOT generate a new UUID merely because an HTTP retry occurs.

If the same logical batch is retried:

> the same `batchId` MUST be reused.

This is the core idempotency requirement.

---

# 7. CRASH / RETRY SEMANTICS

The implementation must correctly handle:

### Scenario A — normal upload

Agent creates batch:

`batchId = X`

Server accepts it.

Agent acknowledges/removes the batch.

Expected:

* Activity rows exist once.

### Scenario B — response lost

Agent sends batch X.

Server commits successfully.

HTTP response is lost.

Agent retries batch X.

Expected:

* no duplicate Activity rows.

### Scenario C — agent crash

Agent sends batch X.

Process crashes before confirming success.

Agent restarts.

Batch X is replayed.

Expected:

* no duplicate Activity rows.

### Scenario D — concurrent retry

Two uploads of batch X reach the server concurrently.

Expected:

* exactly one logical ingestion
* no duplicate Activity rows
* no race-condition error exposed as a failed telemetry upload.

---

# 8. BACKWARD COMPATIBILITY

This is mandatory.

Old agents may send Activity payloads without:

`batchId`

The backend MUST continue accepting them.

Therefore:

### New agent

Payload includes:

* activity records
* batchId
* batchSeq

### Old agent

Payload includes:

* activity records
* no batchId.

Both must be accepted.

Do NOT make `batchId` mandatory at the API level.

Do NOT reject old agents.

Do NOT require a forced agent update.

---

# 9. BACKEND API

Modify the existing:

`POST /api/agent/activity`

Do not create a replacement endpoint.

Do not rename the endpoint.

Do not remove existing response fields.

Accept optional:

`batchId`

Validate it using the repository's existing validation approach.

Do not trust organizationId supplied by the client if authenticated server context already determines organization.

---

# 10. DATABASE MODEL

Add an additive model:

`ActivityBatchReceipt`

Recommended fields:

* `id`
* `organizationId`
* `employeeId`
* `batchId`
* `receivedAt`
* `rowCount`

Required uniqueness:

```text
organizationId + employeeId + batchId
```

Implement as the appropriate Prisma composite unique constraint.

Add indexes needed for:

* organization
* employee
* receivedAt
* retention cleanup.

Do NOT modify or rewrite existing Activity rows.

Do NOT backfill historical Activity records.

Do NOT introduce destructive migration operations.

---

# 11. TRANSACTIONAL INGESTION

This is one of the most important requirements.

The following must be atomic:

1. determine authenticated organization
2. determine authenticated employee
3. validate activity payload
4. check/create receipt
5. insert Activity records
6. commit.

Avoid this broken sequence:

```text
create receipt
↓
transaction fails
↓
activity not inserted
↓
future retry incorrectly treated as duplicate
```

Also avoid:

```text
insert activity
↓
receipt creation fails
↓
retry inserts duplicate activity
```

The receipt and activity ingestion must use the correct transaction strategy.

---

# 12. CONCURRENCY

Explicitly test concurrent duplicate submissions.

Example:

```text
Request A:
org=A
employee=E1
batchId=X

Request B:
org=A
employee=E1
batchId=X
```

Both arrive at approximately the same time.

Expected:

```text
Activity rows = one logical batch
Receipt rows = exactly one
```

The database unique constraint must be treated as part of the concurrency control.

Do not rely solely on:

```text
SELECT receipt
IF NOT EXISTS
INSERT receipt
```

without handling the race.

---

# 13. RESPONSE CONTRACT

For a new batch:

Return the existing successful response contract, extended only if necessary.

For a duplicate batch:

Return HTTP success rather than treating a valid retry as a telemetry failure.

Expose:

`deduplicated`

only if this can be added without breaking existing consumers.

Example conceptual response:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "accepted": 0,
  "deduplicated": 25
}
```

Do NOT remove or rename existing response properties.

Inspect current consumers before changing the response.

---

# 14. IMPORTANT: PARTIAL BATCH VALIDATION

Determine how the current API handles invalid Activity rows.

Do not blindly create a receipt for a batch whose Activity rows were only partially accepted unless that is explicitly the existing contract.

The implementation must define:

* all-or-nothing batch
* partial acceptance
* validation failure
* retry behavior after partial failure.

Preferred behavior where compatible with the current API:

> A batch receipt represents successful ingestion of the complete logical batch.

If the current API intentionally supports partial acceptance, design the receipt semantics around the existing contract instead of breaking it.

Document the decision.

---

# 15. RECEIPT RETENTION

Integrate receipt cleanup into the existing retention system.

Receipts must not grow forever.

Use the same relevant activity retention policy.

For example:

```text
Activity retention = N days
Receipt retention = N days or an explicitly justified equivalent
```

Do not create a second unrelated scheduler.

The cleanup must be:

* tenant-safe
* bounded
* retry-safe
* indexed.

Test deletion of old receipts.

---

# 16. FEATURE FLAG

Inspect the existing settings/feature flag architecture.

If appropriate, add:

`ACTIVITY_DEDUPE`

Semantics:

### OFF

Existing activity ingestion behavior remains available.

### ON

Batch receipt idempotency is enforced.

Do not invent a new feature-flag system.

If the current architecture already provides an equivalent mechanism, use that.

The feature flag must not bypass authentication, organization isolation, validation, or consent.

`src/lib/jobs/settings.ts`

and the existing settings architecture.

`agent_min_version`

It must be optional.

Do not enforce it merely because it exists.

Do not force existing agents to upgrade.

# 18. TEST PLAN

Create tests before declaring Phase 1 complete.

## A. Basic ingestion

Send a new batch.

Assert:

* successful response
* correct Activity count
* one ActivityBatchReceipt
* correct rowCount.

---

## B. Duplicate batch

Send the exact same payload twice with the same batchId.

Assert:

```text
first:
activities inserted > 0

second:
activities inserted = 0
deduplicated = expected count
```

Database must contain exactly one logical Activity set.

---

## C. Concurrent duplicate

Fire two requests concurrently with:

```text
same organization
same employee
same batchId
```

Assert:

* one receipt
* no duplicate activities
* both requests resolve correctly according to the API contract.

---

## D. Response-loss retry

Simulate:

1. server transaction commits
2. client does not receive the response
3. same batch is submitted again.

Assert:

* no duplicates.

---

## E. Agent crash replay

Use the actual agent queue/spool behavior where practical.

Simulate a batch being replayed after restart.

Assert:

* no duplicates.

---

## F. Old-agent compatibility

Send an activity request without:

`batchId`

Assert:

* accepted exactly as before
* no regression in response
* existing behavior preserved.

---

## G. Tenant isolation

Organization A:

```text
employee A
batchId = X
```

Organization B:

```text
employee B
batchId = X
```

Both must be ingested independently.

Expected:

```text
2 receipts
2 independent Activity sets
```

---

## H. Employee isolation

Same organization:

```text
employee A + batch X
employee B + batch X
```

Must NOT dedupe.

---

## I. Race handling

Stress the same batch concurrently.

The implementation must not produce:

* duplicate Activity rows
* duplicate receipt rows
* unhandled unique constraint failures
* cross-tenant leakage.

---

## J. Retention

Create an old receipt.

Run the existing retention mechanism.

Assert:

* old receipt is removed
* recent receipt remains.

            });
          }
      
          // Receipt + rows are committed in ONE transaction: a receipt can never
          // exist without its rows and rows can never exist without their receipt.
          // Race analysis:
          //  - Two identical uploads at once: the second hits the unique
          //    (organizationId, employeeId, batchId) constraint inside its tx,
          //    the whole tx aborts (no partial rows), and the handler re-reads the
          //    winner's committed receipt → success + deduplicated count.
          //  - Retry after a lost response: the first tx committed; the retry takes
          //    the duplicate path above → zero duplicate rows.
          //  - Partial validation failure: validation happens BEFORE this point and
          //    rejects the whole batch (422) — no receipt, no rows.
          const batchIdKey = validBatchId!;
          const receipt = { organizationId, employeeId, batchId: batchIdKey, rowCount: rows.length };
          try {
            await db.$transaction(async (tx) => {
              await tx.activityBatchReceipt.create({ data: receipt });
              await tx.activity.createMany({ data: rows });
            });
            return NextResponse.json({
              success: true,
              count: rows.length,
              deduplicated: 0,
              message: `${rows.length} activities recorded`,
            });
          } catch (error) {
            // The only unique constraint this tx can hit is the batch receipt's
            // (organizationId, employeeId, batchId) key — Activity has no other
            // unique column. Treat that as an idempotent replay, never a 500.
            const isReceiptConflict =
              typeof error === 'object' &&
              error !== null &&
              (error as { code?: string }).code === 'P2002' &&
              JSON.stringify((error as { meta?: { target?: unknown } }).meta?.target ?? '').includes('batchId');
            if (!isReceiptConflict) throw error;
            const existing = await db.activityBatchReceipt.findUnique({
              where: {
                organizationId_employeeId_batchId: {
                  organizationId,
                  employeeId,
                  batchId: batchIdKey,
                },
              },
            });
            if (!existing) throw error; // not a real receipt conflict — surface it
            return NextResponse.json({
              success: true,
              count: 0,
              deduplicated: existing.rowCount,
              message: `${existing.rowCount} activities already recorded for batch ${batchIdKey}`,
            });
          }
        } catch (error) {
          log.error('api.agent.activity.', { error: String('Agent activity error:') }, requestContext(req));
          return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Internal server error' }, { status: 500 });

import type { NativeBridge, ForegroundWindowSample } from './native-bridge';
import { decideConsentGate } from './consent-gate';
import { isWithinWorkingHours, minutesInTimezone } from '../lib/working-hours';
import { isInternalAgentProcess } from '../lib/internal-process';

import type { ActivityQueue } from '../storage/activity-queue';
import type { ActivityCategory, ActivityRecord } from '../types/api';

export interface CollectorState {
  running: boolean;
  lastSampleAt: number | null;
  lastRecordAt: number | null;
  reason: string | null; // why collection is stopped, if any
  collectedToday: number;
}

const UNKNOWN_APP = 'Unknown';

/**
 * Activity collector.
 *
 * Polls the foreground window on a controlled interval (default 10s), tracks
 * the current app, and produces AGGREGATED activity records (one record per
 * contiguous window slice) instead of a record every poll. Fully gated by the
 * consent snapshot for `activity_tracking` — revoked/stale consent stops the
 * collector immediately (the server re-enforces on upload).
 */
export class ActivityCollector {
  private state: CollectorState = {
    running: false,
    lastSampleAt: null,
    lastRecordAt: null,
    reason: null,
    collectedToday: 0,
  };

  private current: { key: string; app: string; title: string | null; startedAt: number } | null = null;

  constructor(
    private readonly native: NativeBridge,
    private readonly consent: ConsentService,
    private readonly config: ConfigService,
    private readonly queue: ActivityQueue,
    private readonly pollMs = 10_000

```json
{
  "path": "docs/PHASE-1-BASELINE.md",
  "instructions": "Document the as-built activity pipeline before Phase 1 dedupe changes.",
  "content": "# PHASE 1 BASELINE — ACTIVITY PIPELINE (AS-BUILT, PRE-DEDUPE)\n\nCaptured 2026-09-03 immediately before Phase 1 implementation, from source\ninspection of `omnisight-agent` and `omnisight-web` (no behavior changed to\nwrite this document). Companion to `docs/V1-IMPLEMENTATION-BASELINE.md`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. Slice creation (agent)\n\n`omnisight-agent/src/collectors/activity-collector.ts`\n\n- Polls the foreground window on a controlled interval (default **10 s**,\n  `pollMs`), tracking the current app key (`app + title`).\n- Produces **aggregated activity records** — one `ActivityRecord` per\n  contiguous foreground-window slice (not one per poll). A slice is flushed\n  when the window changes or on the configured cadence; sub-interval slices\n  under a minimum contiguous duration are aggregated with neighbors.\n- Fully gated agent-side by the `activity_tracking` consent snapshot\n  (`consent-gate.ts`) and working-hours/config gates; a revoked/stale consent\n  stops the collector immediately (the server re-enforces on upload).\n- The website pipeline (`website-collector.ts` / browser-extension native\n  messaging host, plus the native BEST_EFFORT monitor) feeds the SAME queue\n  with `type: 'website'` records (domain-only).\n\n## 2. Queuing (agent)\n\n`omnisight-agent/src/storage/activity-queue.ts`\n\n- `ActivityQueue.enqueue(record)` assigns each queued item a **stable UUID\n  `id`** (generated once at enqueue), an ISO `enqueuedAt`, an `attempts`\n  counter, and the record itself. Items are appended FIFO.\n- The whole queue is **persistent and bounded**: an AES-256-GCM-encrypted\n  JSONL file (plaintext only in test/headless mode), bounded by\n  `maxBytes` (32 MB default; oldest entries trimmed first), crash-safe via\n  atomic tmp+rename rewrites, and fail-closed (an undecryptable/tampered file\n  is quarantined, never parsed, never silently lost).\n\n## 3. Offline persistence / spool semantics\n\n- Queued records survive agent restarts (the file is the spool). Records stay\n  in the file until the server confirms an upload (`ack` deletes them), or a\n  permanent 4xx drop path removes them; `markFailed` increments `attempts`\n  and keeps them.\n\n## 4. Batch construction (agent)\n\n`omnisight-agent/src/services/queue-uploader.ts`\n\n- `QueueUploader.drain()` loops: `queue.peekBatch(maxBatch)` (default **100**,\n  matching the server cap) → upload → ack on success. A drain therefore\n  produces one HTTP request per ≤100-item head slice of the queue.\n- There was **no batch identity** in the payload: each upload was\n  `{ activities: [...] }` (see `src/api/activity.ts`).\n\n## 5. Retry behavior (agent)\n\n- Failed batches are marked (`attempts + 1`) and retained; a 4xx other than\n  429 aborts until the next scheduled drain (it cannot succeed by retrying);\n  5xx/network are retried by later scheduled drains with backoff.\n- **401 is never a permanent failure** (F-01): the batch is retained, auth\n  recovery runs (latched, shared with heartbeat), and the SAME batch is\n  retried with the fresh token. A repeated 401 stops the drain for the next\n  tick — no infinite loop, no data loss.\n\n## 6. Lost HTTP response (agent)\n\n- Delivery is **at-least-once** (documented F-13): the batch is acked only\n  after the server responds. If the response is lost after a successful\n  server commit, the next drain re-uploads the same head items → duplicate\n  Activity rows were possible. Data was never lost, but duplicates could\n  occur.\n\n## 7. Agent crash (agent)\n\n- A crash between the server write and the local ack leaves the items at the\n  head of the persistent queue (their enqueue-time UUID ids intact); on\n  restart the next drain re-uploads them → same duplicate risk as §6.\n\n## 8. Server insertion (web)\n\n`omnisight-web/src/app/api/agent/activity/route.ts` (pre-Phase-1)\n\n- Validates the agent token → derives employee/device; enforces consent\n  (`activity_tracking`, fail-closed), a 1 MB body cap, ≤100 items, per-item\n  allowlist validation (**all-or-nothing**: first invalid item rejects the\n  whole batch 422 — no partial writes), internal-process exclusion, and the\n  org `website_tracking` gate for mixed website batches (whole batch atomic).\n- Then a single `db.activity.createMany(...)` insert (no transaction wrapper;\n  a single statement is atomic by itself, but there was no idempotency key).\n\n## 9. Transaction boundaries (web, pre-Phase-1)\n\n- Validation happened before any write; the insert was one `createMany`\n  (single-statement atomicity). There was no cross-table transaction because\n  no receipt/idempotency record existed. Retries after a lost response or\n  crash could duplicate rows; nothing detected or recorded the replay.\n\n## 10. Organization and employee identity (web, pre-Phase-1)\n\n- Never taken from the client. `validateAgentToken(req)` resolves the\n  `AgentToken` → bound `Device` + `Employee`; the route uses\n  `authResult.employee.id` and `authResult.employee.organizationId`\n  (server-authoritative). `DeviceClaim`/`AgentSession`/consent/working-hours\n  flows were untouched and remain so.\n- Client-supplied `organizationId`/`employeeId` in the body are ignored by\n  the activity route.\n\n---\n\n## Known consequence (the Phase 1 problem)\n\nAt-least-once delivery + no idempotency key = **duplicate Activity rows on\nretry/crash-replay** (documented, accepted pre-Phase-1; F-13). Phase 1 adds a\ntenant-scoped batch receipt so a replayed batch behaves as one logical\ningestion — see `docs/PHASE-1-IMPLEMENTATION.md` and\n`docs/PHASE-1-REPORT.md`.\n"
}
```

**🛠 Write File**

````json
{
  "path": "docs/PHASE-1-IMPLEMENTATION.md",
  "instructions": "Write the Phase 1 implementation documentation per the mandated structure.",
  "content": "# PHASE 1 IMPLEMENTATION — BATCH IDEMPOTENT ACTIVITY INGESTION\n\nReference implementation documentation for the Phase 1 dedupe contract.\nEvidence/results in `docs/PHASE-1-REPORT.md`; as-built pre-change pipeline in\n`docs/PHASE-1-BASELINE.md`.\n\n---\n\n## 1. API\n\n### Old request format (unchanged, still accepted)\n\n```json\n{ \"activities\": [ { \"type\": \"application\", \"applicationName\": \"Code.exe\", \"duration\": 60, ... } ] }\n```\n\n### New request format (both fields OPTIONAL)\n\n```json\n{\n  \"activities\": [ ... ],\n  \"batchId\": \"3f8a…-… (RFC-4122 UUID v1–v5)\",\n  \"batchSeq\": 1\n}\n```\n\n### Compatibility\n\n- No `batchId` (old agents) → the route runs **exactly today's path**: no\n  receipt is consulted or written; response is the legacy shape\n  `{ success, count, message }` (no `deduplicated` key).\n- `batchId` present but the org flag off → same legacy path (key ignored).\n- `batchId` malformed, or `batchSeq` not a non-negative safe integer → `422`\n  before any write. Unknown/extra fields are ignored.\n- No endpoint renamed or replaced; no existing response property removed or\n  renamed.\n\n### Response behavior\n\n| Scenario | Status | Body |\n|---|---|---|\n| Legacy (no batchId) / flag off | 200 | `{ success, count, message }` |\n| New batch, flag on | 200 | `{ success, count: N, deduplicated: 0, message }` |\n| Duplicate batch, flag on | 200 | `{ success, count: 0, deduplicated: <rowCount>, message }` |\n| Consent missing / website gate | 403 | unchanged, whole batch atomic |\n| Validation failure | 422 | unchanged (all-or-nothing batch) |\n\n`deduplicated` is added only on the dedupe path; existing consumers of\n`success`/`count`/`message` are unaffected.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Database\n\n`ActivityBatchReceipt` (additive model, `prisma/schema.prisma`):\n\n| Column | Type | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `id` | cuid PK | |\n| `organizationId` | FK → Organization (Cascade) | own tenant column (unlike Activity) |\n| `employeeId` | FK → Employee (Cascade) | |\n| `batchId` | string | UUID from the agent |\n| `receivedAt` | timestamptz, default now | |\n| `rowCount` | int | rows actually ingested for the batch |\n\n- **Unique:** `@@unique([organizationId, employeeId, batchId])` — the concurrency arbiter and the isolation boundary.\n- **Indexes:** `(organizationId, receivedAt)` for org retention cleanup, `(employeeId)`, `(receivedAt)`.\n- **Migration:** `prisma/migrations/20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts/migration.sql` — pure additive `CREATE TABLE` + unique + indexes + FKs. Verified drift-free (`prisma migrate diff` → \"No difference detected\") and applies cleanly (`migrate deploy` on scratch DB).\n- **Retention:** receipts are purged by the EXISTING org retention sweep\n  (`src/lib/jobs/retention.ts`, `runRetentionForOrg`) using the org's\n  `activity_retention_days` window, keyed on `receivedAt` and indexed\n  `(organizationId, receivedAt)`. 0 days (never purge) is honored like every\n  other retention key. No second scheduler was created.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Agent\n\n- **Batch generation:** `QueueUploader` derives ONE `batchId` per upload batch\n  via `deriveBatchId(itemIds)` — a deterministic UUID v5 over the sorted UUIDs\n  of the queued items in the batch. Never one per row. `batchSeq` is a\n  monotonic per-process counter attached to each upload attempt.\n- **Retry semantics:** the same queued items always derive the same\n  `batchId`, so an HTTP retry (including the 401-recovery retry of the same\n  batch) reuses the id automatically — no regeneration on retry.\n- **Crash replay:** item ids persist in the queue file at enqueue, so a new\n  process (restart) draining the same head items derives the SAME `batchId`.\n  Proven by unit test P1-3 (new uploader instance over the same queue file).\n- **Collection behavior is unchanged**: `activity-collector.ts`,\n  `website-collector.ts`, consent/working-hours gating and slice aggregation\n  were not modified. The queue/spool format is untouched (ids already existed).\n\n---\n\n## 4. Transactions\n\nExact atomicity strategy in `src/app/api/agent/activity/route.ts`:\n\n```text\nauthenticate (token) → consent gate → body cap → per-item validation (whole\nbatch 422 on first invalid item) → internal-process filter → website gate +\ndomain normalization →  [flag ON + batchId]\ndb.$transaction(\n  tx.activityBatchReceipt.create({ organizationId, employeeId, batchId, rowCount })\n  tx.activity.createMany(rows)\n)\n```\n\nBoth writes commit or abort together, so neither of these can happen:\n\n- receipt committed without its activity rows (would poison future retries), or\n- activity rows committed without a receipt (would let a retry duplicate).\n\n`rowCount` = the rows actually ingested (post existing deterministic\nfilters), so a receipt always represents successful ingestion of the complete\nlogical batch under the current all-or-nothing contract.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Concurrency\n\n- The DB unique constraint is the concurrency control (not check-then-insert).\n- Two simultaneous identical uploads: one transaction commits; the loser's\n  `create` raises P2002 inside its transaction → the whole transaction aborts\n  (Postgres: no partial rows) → the handler re-reads the winner's committed\n  receipt on a fresh statement → `200 { deduplicated: rowCount }`.\n- The client never sees a constraint failure — a valid retry is a success.\n- Same `batchId` across orgs/employees is two independent keys (tests G/H).\n\n---\n\n## 6. Feature flag\n\n- No new flag framework: reuses the existing org-scoped `OrganizationSetting`\n  registry + `getOrgSetting` resolver (`src/lib/jobs/settings.ts`).\n- Key `activity_dedupe` (`ACTIVITY_DEDUPE_SETTING_KEY`), **default OFF**.\n  - OFF → legacy ingestion (receipts never consulted/written).\n  - ON → receipt-based dedupe for uploads that carry a `batchId`.\n- Server-side only: never shipped to agents (the agent config route selects\n  keys explicitly) and not shown in agent-monitoring toggles.\n- The flag cannot bypass auth, org isolation, validation, or consent — it\n  only wraps the final insert.\n- `agent_min_version` (`AGENT_MIN_VERSION_SETTING_KEY`, optional org-scoped\n  string, `resolveAgentMinVersion`): **informational capability marker only**.\n  Version is obtained from `agentVersion` reported on `/api/agent/discover`\n  and stored on `Device.agentVersion`. Nothing compares or enforces it in\n  Phase 1 — unset/blank = no floor; older agents are never rejected. Future\n  phases may gate additively against this floor.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Rollback\n\n1. Turn the flag OFF (delete/set `OrganizationSetting.activity_dedupe`) →\n   ingestion returns to legacy behavior immediately; existing receipts become\n   inert and age out via retention.\n2. Revert the additive code (route/retention/settings/agent).\n3. If migration rollback is required: `DROP TABLE \"ActivityBatchReceipt\"` is\n   safe — the table is new, no existing row references it, and no code path\n   depends on it once the flag is off. Existing `Activity` rows are never\n   touched by any step.\n"
}
````

{
  "path": "docs/PHASE-1-REPORT.md",
  "instructions": "Rewrite the Phase 1 report in the mandated 22-section structure.",
  "content": "# PHASE 1 REPORT — RELIABLE TELEMETRY\n\n```\nStatus: GREEN\nDate:   2026-09-03\n```\n\n---\n\n## 1. Executive Summary\n\nActivity uploads can no longer duplicate Activity rows when a logical batch is\nretried, replayed after a crash, or submitted concurrently — while every old\nagent, old server, and org with the feature flag off keeps today's exact\nbehavior. The agent attaches a **deterministic, retry-stable `batchId`** (one\nper logical batch, plus a monotonic `batchSeq`) to each upload; the web backend\nwrites an **`ActivityBatchReceipt` in the same transaction as the rows** when\nthe org-scoped `activity_dedupe` flag is on, and answers replays with HTTP 200\nplus an accurate `deduplicated` count. Dedupe identity is scoped to\n`organizationId + employeeId + batchId` — never `batchId` alone. Receipts are\npurged by the existing retention sweep on the activity window. No Phase 2+\nfeature was implemented.\n\n## 2. Baseline\n\nPhase 0 is GREEN (see `docs/PHASE-0-REPORT.md`): web 96/96 suites, 1561\nsubtests, typecheck/build/lint (0 errors) PASS; agent 625/625 tests, typecheck,\nbuild PASS; no hardcoded credentials; bun-canonical lockfile; `.next/dev/types`\nguarded. The pre-change activity pipeline is documented in\n`docs/PHASE-1-BASELINE.md` (collector → encrypted persistent queue → at-least-\nonce uploader → single `createMany`, no idempotency key). Phase 0 behavior was\nnot modified during Phase 1.\n\n## 3. Architecture Before\n\nAgent: 10 s foreground-window polls → contiguous slices → one `ActivityRecord`\nper slice → FIFO encrypted queue (bounded, crash-safe; each item carries a\nstable UUID `id`) → `QueueUploader.drain()` uploads ≤100-item head slices as\n`{ activities }` → acks only on server response. Server: token/consent/website\ngates → all-or-nothing per-item validation → single `db.activity.createMany`.\nAt-least-once delivery with no idempotency key meant a lost response or crash\nbetween server commit and local ack re-uploaded the same rows (duplicates\npossible, data never lost).\n\n## 4. Architecture After\n\nAgent: `attemptUpload` derives `batchId = deriveBatchId(item ids)` (UUID v5\nover the batch's sorted queued-item ids — stable for the same items, distinct\nfor different sets) plus monotonic `batchSeq`, and posts\n`{ activities, batchId, batchSeq }`. Server: when `batchId` is present AND the\norg's `activity_dedupe` flag is on, the receipt insert and the row insert run\nin one `db.$transaction`; a P2002 on the unique key takes the replay path\n(fresh read of the committed receipt → 200 + `deduplicated`). Flag off or no\n`batchId` → legacy path unchanged.\n\n## 5. Agent Changes\n\n`omnisight-agent`:\n- `src/api/activity.ts` — `upload(activities, meta?: ActivityUploadMeta)`;\n  attaches `batchId`/`batchSeq` when meta is provided (new optional exported\n  `ActivityUploadMeta`).\n- `src/services/queue-uploader.ts` — exported `deriveBatchId(itemIds)` and a\n  monotonic per-process `batchSeq`; every upload attempt (including\n  401-recovery retries) sends meta. F-13 comment updated to the dedupe\n  contract.\n- `src/types/api.ts` — `ActivityUploadResponse.deduplicated?: number`\n  (additive).\n- `tests/queue-uploader.test.ts` — fake captures meta; +3 tests (deriveBatchId\n  determinism; stable id + monotonic seq across a failed-then-successful\n  drain; **crash replay: a NEW uploader instance over the same persisted queue\n  derives the same batchId**).\n- Collector/slice/queue formats untouched.\n\n## 6. API Changes\n\n`POST /api/agent/activity` (no rename; no field removed). Request fields\n`batchId` (optional RFC-4122 UUID v1–v5; malformed → 422) and `batchSeq`\n(optional non-negative safe integer; malformed → 422) added. When dedupe\napplies, responses add a `deduplicated` key; otherwise the response shape is\nidentical to before. Full tables in `docs/PHASE-1-IMPLEMENTATION.md` §1.\n\n## 7. Database Changes\n\nNew additive model `ActivityBatchReceipt` (`id`, `organizationId`, `employeeId`,\n`batchId`, `receivedAt`, `rowCount`) with `@@unique([organizationId,\nemployeeId, batchId])` and indexes `(organizationId, receivedAt)`,\n`(employeeId)`, `(receivedAt)`; back-relations added on `Organization` and\n`Employee`. Migration `20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts` (new table +\nunique + indexes + FKs only). No existing Activity row was modified or\nbackfilled; no destructive operations.\n\n## 8. Transaction Strategy\n\nReceipt + rows commit atomically in one interactive `db.$transaction`\n(`tx.activityBatchReceipt.create` then `tx.activity.createMany`). This makes\n\"receipt without rows\" and \"rows without receipt\" both impossible. Validation\nhappens before the transaction, so an invalid batch never creates a receipt.\n`rowCount` = rows actually ingested after the existing deterministic filters\n(= successful ingestion of the complete logical batch under the all-or-nothing\ncontract).\n\n## 9. Concurrency Strategy\n\nThe DB unique constraint is the concurrency control. A loser's P2002 aborts\nits whole transaction (no partial rows); the handler re-reads the winner's\nreceipt on a fresh statement and returns success. Tested with two simultaneous\nidentical uploads (one insert + one dedupe, one receipt, both clients 200) and\ndocumented for retry-after-lost-response, crash replay, and mixed batches.\n\n## 10. Backward Compatibility\n\n- Old agents (no `batchId`): accepted; legacy insertion + legacy response\n  shape; duplicate-on-replay semantics unchanged (proven by test P1-5).\n- Old servers: ignore the new optional fields (agent keeps working).\n- Orgs with the flag off: receipts never written/consulted (test P1-8).\n- No forced agent update; Phase 1 works with zero agent upgrades (old payload\n  is a first-class citizen).\n\n## 11. Retention\n\n`runRetentionForOrg` now purges receipts older than the org's\n`activity_retention_days` window via indexed `(organizationId, receivedAt)`\ndelete — same sweep, same window, same 0 = never-purge rule as other keys.\nResult field `activityBatchReceipts` added (interface + empty results).\nTest P1-10: backdated receipt removed, fresh receipt kept.\n\n## 12. Feature Flag\n\nOrg-scoped `OrganizationSetting` key `activity_dedupe`\n(`ACTIVITY_DEDUPE_SETTING_KEY` in `src/lib/jobs/settings.ts`, resolved by\n`resolveActivityDedupeEnabled`), default **OFF**. OFF = legacy ingestion\n(available unchanged); ON = receipt dedupe for batches that carry a\n`batchId`. Server-side only; never shipped in the agent config payload; cannot\nbypass auth/isolation/validation/consent. Optional informational\n`agent_min_version` capability marker added with `resolveAgentMinVersion`\n(unset = no floor; nothing enforces it yet — future additive gating only).\n\n## 13. Security Verification\n\n- batchId cannot cross orgs/employees: server derives org+employee from the\n  authenticated `AgentToken`; unique key is tenant+employee scoped (tests\n  P1-6/P1-7).\n- Unauthenticated requests are rejected by `validateAgentToken` before any\n  receipt logic (no receipt possible without auth).\n- Expired sessions/devices follow the existing token lifecycle — untouched.\n- `DeviceClaim`/`AgentSession`/consent/working-hours/break enforcement,\n  rate limiting, and client-`organizationId`-ignored semantics: unchanged\n  (route edits sit strictly after the existing gates).\n- Web full gate (incl. security/agent/org-isolation suites): green.\n\n## 14. Privacy Verification\n\nNo new collection: receipts carry only id/tenant/employee/batch key/row count/\ntime — never titles, URLs, domains, or content. Consent gates and\nbreak-mode/working-hours suppression untouched. Receipts age out under the\nactivity retention window. No raw keys, emails, or webcam content involved.\n\n## 15. Performance / Index Review\n\n- Receipt lookup/conflict: unique-index probe + one `findUnique` by the\n  compound key — no table scan.\n- Insert path: unchanged `createMany` on existing indexes.\n- Retention: indexed org+receivedAt delete in the scheduled per-org sweep.\n- Growth estimate (100 employees, ~1 activity/min × 8 h ≈ 48k activity\n  rows/day/org): receipts = one per accepted batch (≤100 rows). With a\n  realistic drain cadence this is roughly hundreds/day/org — on the order of\n  ~45k receipts at 90-day retention vs ~4.3 M activity rows (~1%). Retention\n  keeps it bounded; verified `prisma migrate diff` reports no drift.\n\n## 16. Tests Executed\n\n- Web new suite `tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts` (11 tests): basic; duplicate;\n  concurrent duplicate (Promise.all); response-loss retry; legacy payload;\n  tenant isolation; employee isolation; flag-off semantics; malformed\n  batchId/batchSeq → 422; retention purge/keep; cross-repo static contract.\n- Agent `tests/queue-uploader.test.ts` (+3): deriveBatchId determinism/shape;\n  stable batchId + monotonic batchSeq across failed→successful drains;\n  crash-replay across uploader instances.\n- Targeted web regressions: website-tracking, website-100, agent-hardening,\n  agent-cross-org-attack, consent-summary, super-admin, audio,\n  anomaly-hardening, break-hardening, hardening (all exit 0).\n- Full gates: web `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`,\n  97-suite runner; agent `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, `npm run build`.\n\n## 17. Exact Test Results\n\n- `tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts`: **11/11 PASS** (exit 0).\n- Web full suite (5 sequential chunks, per-file `node --import tsx --test`,\n  identical to `scripts/run-tests.mjs`): **97/97 suites, 1573/1573 subtests\n  pass, 0 fail** (Phase 0's 96 suites remain green; +1 new dedupe suite).\n- Web `npm run typecheck`: PASS (exit 0). Web `npm run lint`: **0 errors**\n  (439 warnings, unchanged class). Web `npm run build`: PASS (exit 0).\n- Agent `npm test`: **628/628 PASS** (baseline 625 + 3 new). Agent typecheck:\n  PASS. Agent build: PASS.\n\n## 18. Files Changed\n\nWeb: `prisma/schema.prisma`, `prisma/migrations/20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts/migration.sql`,\n`src/app/api/agent/activity/route.ts`, `src/lib/jobs/settings.ts`,\n`src/lib/jobs/retention.ts`, `src/lib/jobs/run.ts`, `tests/activity-dedupe.test.ts`.\nAgent: `src/api/activity.ts`, `src/services/queue-uploader.ts`,\n`src/types/api.ts`, `tests/queue-uploader.test.ts`.\nDocs: `docs/PHASE-1-BASELINE.md`, `docs/PHASE-1-IMPLEMENTATION.md`,\n`docs/PHASE-1-REPORT.md` (this file).\n\n## 19. Migration\n\n`20260903000000_activity_batch_receipts` — additive `CREATE TABLE`\n`ActivityBatchReceipt` + compound unique + 3 indexes + 2 cascading FKs.\nNaming follows the repo's `YYYYMMDDHHMMSS_name` convention. Verified:\n`prisma validate` PASS; `prisma migrate deploy` on a scratch DB PASS; all\nmigrations applied; `prisma migrate diff --from-migrations → schema` =\n**No difference detected**; existing Activity rows untouched (no backfill, no\nalterations).\n\n## 20. Rollback\n\n1. Flag OFF (delete/set `OrganizationSetting.activity_dedupe`) → legacy\n   ingestion immediately; receipts inert and aged out by retention.\n2. Revert additive code (route, retention, settings, agent files).\n3. Migration rollback = `DROP TABLE \"ActivityBatchReceipt\"` (safe: new table,\n   nothing references it once the flag is off; Activity data untouched).\nOld agents/servers unaffected at every step.\n\n## 21. Remaining Risks\n\n- Dedupe is opt-in per org (default off). Orgs that don't enable it keep\n  documented at-least-once semantics until enabled.\n- `batchSeq` is per-process monotonic (resets on agent restart) — sufficient\n  for its informational purpose; persistence would be needed for cross-restart\n  ordering.\n- `agent_min_version` is intentionally inert in Phase 1 (no feature needs it;\n  enforcement would break the no-upgrade guarantee).\n- Two log lines added per dedupe batch (`batch-received` /\n  `batch-duplicate`) — bounded, no content, no per-row logging.\n\n## 22. Final Verdict\n\n> **GREEN** — all Phase 1 acceptance criteria verified with executable\n> evidence: agent generates one retry-stable batchId per logical batch with a\n> monotonic batchSeq and unchanged collection; old agents remain compatible;\n> `POST /api/agent/activity` accepts optional batchId with server-authoritative\n> org/employee scoping; `ActivityBatchReceipt` with composite uniqueness and\n> appropriate indexes exists; ingestion is transactionally atomic; concurrent\n> and replay duplicates produce zero duplicate rows with no error exposed;\n> retention cleanup exists and is tested; tenant and employee isolation are\n> proven; full web (97/97 suites, 1573 subtests, typecheck, lint 0 errors,\n> build) and agent (628/628, typecheck, build) regression gates are green.\n"
}

- path: src\app\api\settings\monitoring\route.ts
  content: |
      import { NextRequest, NextResponse } from 'next/server';
      import { db } from '@/lib/db';
      import { authenticateRequest, getSessionOrg, requireManagerOrg, authError } from '@/lib/api';
      import { hasRolePermission } from '@/lib/auth';
      import {
        MONITORING_KEYS,
        validateMonitoringValue,
        coerceMonitoringValue,
      } from '@/lib/jobs/settings';
      import type { MonitoringKey } from '@/lib/jobs/settings';
      import { log, requestContext } from '@/lib/logger';
      
      // Org-scoped agent monitoring configuration. Values are persisted in the
      // OrganizationSetting table (one row per org + key) and consumed by
      // GET /api/agent/config — which the desktop agent syncs and applies to its
      // scheduler at runtime (no agent restart required).
      //
      // S-1 / MON-1: there is NO fallback to the global SystemSetting. Every key is
      // validated against the typed MONITORING_KEYS registry in src/lib/jobs/settings
      // — booleans (true/false), whole numbers within the configured range, and
      // 24-hour HH:MM times. Unknown keys are rejected outright.
      
      // GET /api/settings/monitoring — current monitoring configuration for the org
      // with validation metadata (type, default, min/max) so the UI renders the
      // right control without duplicating any validation rules.
      // Manager+ (defense-in-depth: monitoring config reveals agent scheduling behavior).
      export async function GET(req: NextRequest) {
        try {
          const scope = await requireManagerOrg(req);
          if (!scope.ok) {
            return authError(scope);
          }
      
          const rows = await db.organizationSetting.findMany({
            where: { organizationId: scope.organizationId, key: { in: Object.keys(MONITORING_KEYS) } },
          });
          const stored = new Map(rows.map((r) => [r.key, r.value]));
      
          const settings = (Object.keys(MONITORING_KEYS) as MonitoringKey[]).map((key) => {
            const def = MONITORING_KEYS[key];
            const raw = stored.get(key);
            const validated = raw !== undefined ? validateMonitoringValue(key, raw) : null;
            return {
              key,
              value: validated?.ok ? coerceMonitoringValue(key, validated.value) : def.default,
              type: def.type,
              default: def.default,
              // Numeric bounds only exist for `number` keys.
              min: 'min' in def ? def.min : undefined,
              max: 'max' in def ? def.max : undefined,
            };
          });
      
          return NextResponse.json({ data: settings });
        } catch (error) {
          log.error('api.settings.monitoring.', { error: String('Monitoring settings GET error:') }, requestContext(req));
          return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Failed to fetch monitoring settings' }, { status: 500 });
        }
      }
      
      // PUT /api/settings/monitoring — update one monitoring setting (admin+).
      // Validated against the typed registry, tenant-scoped, and audited.
      export async function PUT(req: NextRequest) {
        try {
          const auth = await authenticateRequest(req);
          if (!auth) {
            return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Unauthorized. Please sign in.' }, { status: 401 });
          }
          if (!hasRolePermission(auth.role, 'admin')) {
            return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Insufficient permissions' }, { status: 403 });
          }
          const org = await getSessionOrg(req);
          if (!org) return NextResponse.json({ error: 'No organization found' }, { status: 404 });
      
          const body = await req.json();
          const { key, value } = body as { key?: string; value?: unknown };
      
          if (!key || !(key in MONITORING_KEYS)) {
            return NextResponse.json(
              { error: `Invalid monitoring key. Valid: ${Object.keys(MONITORING_KEYS).join(', ')}` },
              { status: 400 }
            );
          }
      
          // Central typed validation: booleans, whole numbers in range, HH:MM times.
          const validation = validateMonitoringValue(key as MonitoringKey, value);
          if (!validation.ok) {
            return NextResponse.json({ error: validation.error }, { status: 422 });
          }
      
          const setting = await db.$transaction(async (tx) => {
            const upserted = await tx.organizationSetting.upsert({
              where: { organizationId_key: { organizationId: org.id, key } },
              update: { value: validation.value, category: 'monitoring' },
              create: { organizationId: org.id, key, value: validation.value, category: 'monitoring' },
            });
            await tx.auditLog.create({
              data: {
                action: 'configure',
                resource: 'settings',
                resourceId: upserted.id,
                description: `Agent monitoring setting ${key} set to ${validation.value} by ${auth.email}`,
                userId: auth.userId,
                organizationId: org.id,
              },
            });
            return upserted;
          });
      
          return NextResponse.json({ data: { key, value: validation.value }, setting });
        } catch (error) {
          log.error('api.settings.monitoring.', { error: String('Monitoring settings PUT error:') }, requestContext(req));
          return NextResponse.json({ error: 'Failed to update monitoring setting' }, { status: 500 });
        }
      }
      
  referencedBy: {}
